Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Aizu has an ancient legend of buried treasure. You have finally found the place where the buried treasure is buried. Since we know the depth of the buried treasure and the condition of the strata to be dug, we can reach the buried treasure at the lowest cost with careful planning. So you decided to create a program that reads the condition of the formation and calculates the route to reach the buried treasure depth at the lowest cost.

The state of the formation is represented by cells arranged in a two-dimensional grid, and the position of each cell is represented by the coordinates (x, y). Let the upper left be (1,1), and assume that the x coordinate increases as it goes to the right and the y coordinate increases as it goes deeper down. You choose one of the cells with the smallest y-coordinate and start digging from there, then dig into one of the cells with the largest y-coordinate. There are two types of cells in the formation:

1. A cell filled with soil. There is a fixed cost for each cell to dig.
2. Oxygen-filled cell. There is no need to dig, and each cell can be replenished with a fixed amount of oxygen. The oxygen in the cell that has been replenished with oxygen is exhausted and cannot be replenished again. Also, when you reach this cell, you must replenish oxygen.



Only left, right, and down cells can be dug from a cell. Once you have dug a cell, you can move it left or right, but you cannot move it up.

You must carry an oxygen cylinder with you when excavating. The moment the oxygen cylinder reaches zero, you will not be able to move, excavate, or replenish oxygen. The remaining amount is decremented by 1 each time you move the cell. Even if the remaining amount of the oxygen cylinder is 0 and the depth of the buried treasure is reached, it is not considered to have been reached. In addition, oxygen can be replenished in cells that have accumulated oxygen, but the excess capacity is discarded.

Create a program that inputs the size of the formation, the excavation cost, the capacity of the oxygen cylinder, the amount of oxygen in the initial state, and the information of the formation, and outputs the minimum cost to reach the deepest cell. However, if the minimum cost exceeds the excavation cost, or if you cannot reach the buried treasure no matter how you dig, please output "NA".

<image>



Input

A sequence of multiple datasets is given as input. The end of the input is indicated by two zero lines. Each dataset is given in the following format.


W H
f m o
c1,1 c2,1 ... cW,1
c1,2 c2,2 ... cW,2
...
c1, H c2, H ... cW, H


The horizontal size W of the formation and the vertical size H (3 ≤ W, H ≤ 10) are given in the first line. The second line is the integer f (1 ≤ f ≤ 10000) that represents your excavation cost, the integer m (3 ≤ m ≤ 50) that represents the capacity of the oxygen cylinder, and the integer o that represents the amount of oxygen you have in the initial state. o ≤ m) is given.

The following H line is given the geological information ci, j. ci, j represents the cell information for coordinates (i, j) and is given in the following format:
If the value is negative, the cell is full of soil and the value represents the cost.
If the value is positive, it is a cell filled with oxygen, and the value represents the amount of oxygen.
However, there are no more than 50 cells in which oxygen has accumulated.

The number of datasets does not exceed 50.

Output

Print the minimum cost or NA on one line for each dataset.

3 3

-100 -20 -100
-100 -20 -100

100 10 10

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a 2D grid of cells with coordinates (x,y) where (1,1) is top-left and y increases downward
2. We start at the cell with smallest y-coordinate (top row)
3. We must reach the cell with largest y-coordinate (bottom row) - this is our target
4. We can only move left, right, or down (no up movement)
5. Cells have two types:
   - Soil cells: have a digging cost
   - Oxygen cells: have oxygen capacity (can replenish oxygen, but only once per cell)
6. We carry an oxygen cylinder that starts with `o` units
7. Oxygen decreases by 1 for each move (including when moving to a cell)
8. When we reach an oxygen cell, we must replenish our oxygen (up to capacity `m`)
9. We need to find the minimum total digging cost to reach any cell in the bottom row
10. If the minimum cost exceeds `f` (max excavation cost) or if it's impossible, output "NA"

## Key Insights

1. This is a shortest path problem with state space that includes position and oxygen level
2. We need to track: (x, y, oxygen_level)
3. From each state, we can move to adjacent cells (left, right, down)
4. When we reach an oxygen cell, we replenish oxygen (to capacity)
5. We need to reach any cell in the bottom row (y = H)
6. We must ensure we never run out of oxygen (oxygen level >= 0 at all times)

## Algorithm Approach

1. Use BFS or Dijkstra since we have non-negative costs (digging costs)
2. State: (x, y, oxygen_level)
3. Start from all cells in row 1 (y=1) with initial oxygen level `o`
4. For each valid move, add to queue with updated state
5. When we reach any cell with y = H, return the total cost
6. If we can't reach any bottom cell or the cost exceeds f, return "NA"

## Implementation Steps

1. Parse input
2. Create a queue for BFS/Dijkstra with states (cost, x, y, oxygen)
3. For each state, explore neighbors (left, right, down)
4. Handle oxygen replenishment when entering oxygen cells
5. Track visited states to avoid cycles
6. Return minimum cost when reaching bottom row

```python
from collections import defaultdict
import heapq
import sys

def solve():
    while True:
        line = input().strip()
        if line == "0 0":
            break
            
        W, H = map(int, line.split())
        f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
        
        grid = []
        for _ in range(H):
            row = list(map(int, input().split()))
            grid.append(row)
        
        # Special handling: if we can't reach bottom row at all
        # We need to check if there's any path from top to bottom
        
        # Dijkstra with state (cost, x, y, oxygen)
        # Priority queue: (total_cost, x, y, oxygen)
        pq = []
        
        # Start from all cells in top row (y=0 in 0-indexed, which is y=1 in 1-indexed)
        # But we can only start from cells with smallest y-coordinate (top row)
        for x in range(W):
            # Start at (x, 0) in 0-indexed, with initial oxygen o
            heapq.heappush(pq, (0, x, 0, o))
        
        # Visited set: (x, y, oxygen_level) -> min_cost
        visited = {}
        
        min_cost = float('inf')
        
        while pq:
            cost, x, y, oxygen = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            # If we reached the bottom row (y = H-1 in 0-indexed)
            if y == H - 1:
                min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
                continue
                
            # Skip if we already have a better cost for this state
            state = (x, y, oxygen)
            if state in visited and visited[state] <= cost:
                continue
            visited[state] = cost
            
            # Generate next moves: left, right, down
            for dx, dy in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
                nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
                
                # Check bounds
                if ny >= H or nx < 0 or nx >= W:
                    continue
                    
                # Skip if we can't move up or the move is invalid
                # Since we can only move down, left, right, not up
                # So we need to ensure we don't go up
                
                # Check if we have enough oxygen to make this move
                if oxygen <= 0:
                    continue
                    
                # Move to (nx, ny)
                new_oxygen = oxygen - 1
                
                # Get cell value
                cell_value = grid[ny][nx]
                
                # If cell has soil, add cost
                if cell_value < 0:
                    new_cost = cost + abs(cell_value)
                    # If new cost already exceeds max excavation cost, skip
                    if new_cost > f:
                        continue
                    # Add to priority queue
                    new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen)
                    if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
                        heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen))
                
                # If cell has oxygen, we must replenish oxygen
                elif cell_value > 0:
                    # We can only replenish if we have oxygen to begin with
                    # After moving, we have new_oxygen (which is oxygen-1)
                    # But when we reach this cell, we replenish to capacity m
                    # So final oxygen becomes min(new_oxygen + cell_value, m)
                    # But note: the problem says "when you reach this cell, you must replenish oxygen"
                    # And "the oxygen in the cell that has been replenished... is exhausted"
                    # So we consume 1 oxygen for the move, then replenish with cell_value
                    # But the replenishment is limited to capacity m
                    new_oxygen_after_replenish = min(new_oxygen + cell_value, m)
                    new_cost = cost
                    
                    # Check if new cost exceeds max excavation cost
                    if new_cost > f:
                        continue
                        
                    new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen_after_replenish)
                    if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
                        heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen_after_replenish))
        
        # After processing, if we found a valid path to bottom row
        if min_cost != float('inf'):
            print(min_cost)
        else:
            print("NA")

solve()
```

```python
from collections import defaultdict

def solve():
    while True:
        line = input().strip()
        if line == "0 0":
            break
            
        W, H = map(int, line.split())
        f, m, o = map(int, input().split())

            heapq.heappush(pq, (0, x, 0, o))

        visited = {}
        
        min_cost = float('inf')
        
        while pq:
            cost, x, y, oxygen = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            # If we reached the bottom row
            if y == H - 1:
                min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
                # We don't break because there might be multiple paths to bottom
                # But we continue to find the minimum
            
            # Skip if we have a better cost for this state
            state = (x, y, oxygen)
            if state in visited and visited[state] <= cost:
                continue
            visited[state] = cost
            
            # Try all valid moves: left, right, down
            for dx, dy in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
                nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
                
                # Check bounds
                if ny >= H or nx < 0 or nx >= W:
                    continue
                    
                # We can only move down, left, right (no up)
                # But we need to ensure we don't try to go up
                
                # Check if we have enough oxygen for the move
                if oxygen <= 0:
                    continue
                    
                # Move to (nx, ny) - we consume 1 oxygen
                new_oxygen = oxygen - 1
                new_cost = cost
                
                # Get the cell value at (nx, ny)
                cell_value = grid[ny][nx]
                
                # If it's soil, we have to pay the cost
                if cell_value < 0:
                    new_cost += abs(cell_value)
                    # If cost exceeds max excavation cost, skip
                    if new_cost > f:
                        continue
                
                # If it's oxygen cell, we replenish when we reach it
                if cell_value > 0:
                    # When we reach this cell, we replenish our oxygen
                    # But only if we have oxygen left after the move?
                    # Wait, the problem says "when you reach this cell, you must replenish oxygen"
                    # This happens after we move to the cell
                    # So we replenish after consuming oxygen for the move
                    # The replenishment adds cell_value to our oxygen, but capped at m
                    new_oxygen = min(new_oxygen + cell_value, m)
                    # But we already consumed 1 oxygen for the move
                    # So the new oxygen is (oxygen - 1) + cell_value, capped at m
                    
                # Add this state to the queue
                new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen)
                if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
                    heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen))
        
        if min_cost == float('inf'):
            print("NA")
        else:
            print(min_cost)
```

```python
import heapq
from collections import defaultdict

while True:
    line = input().strip()
    if line == "0 0":
        break
        
    W, H = map(int, line.split())
    f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
    
    grid = []
    for _ in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        grid.append(row)
    
    # Dijkstra: (cost, x, y, oxygen)
    pq = []
    
    # Start from all cells in top row (y=0)
    for x in range(W):
        heapq.heappush(pq, (0, x, 0, o))
    
    # Dictionary to store minimum cost for each state (x, y, oxygen)
    visited = {}
    min_cost = float('inf')
    
    while pq:
        cost, x, y, oxygen = heapq.heappop(pq)
        
        # If we reached the bottom row (y = H-1), update minimum cost
        if y == H - 1:
            min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
        
        # Skip if we have a better cost for this state
        state = (x, y, oxygen)
        if state in visited and visited[state] <= cost:
            continue
        visited[state] = cost
        
        # Try all possible moves: left, right, down
        for dx, dy in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
            nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
            
            # Check bounds
            if ny >= H or nx < 0 or nx >= W:
                continue
                
            # We can only move down, left, right (no up)
            # But we're already generating only valid directions
            
            # Check if we have enough oxygen to make this move
            if oxygen <= 0:
                continue
                
            # Moving to (nx, ny) consumes 1 oxygen
            new_oxygen = oxygen - 1
            new_cost = cost
            
            # Get the cell value in (nx, ny)
            cell_value = grid[ny][nx]
            
            # If it's soil (negative value), we must pay the digging cost
            if cell_value < 0:
                new_cost += abs(cell_value)
                # If new cost exceeds max excavation cost, skip
                if new_cost > f:
                    continue
            
            # If it's an oxygen cell (positive value), we replenish when we reach it
            if cell_value > 0:
                # When we reach this cell, we replenish our oxygen
                new_oxygen = min(new_oxygen + cell_value, m)
            
            # Create new state
            new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen)
            
            # If we haven't seen this state or have a better cost, add to queue
            if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
                heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen))
    
    if min_cost == float('inf'):
        print("NA")
    else:
        print(min_cost)
```

```python
import heapq

while True:
    line = input().strip()
    if line == "0 0":
        break
        
    W, H = map(int, line.split())
    f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
    
    grid = []
    for _ in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        grid.append(row)
    
    # Dijkstra: (cost, x, y, oxygen)
    pq = []
    
    # Start from all cells in top row (y=0)
    for x in range(W):
        # We start by digging cell (x,0), so we pay cost for that cell
        initial_cost = abs(grid[0][x]) if grid[0][x] < 0 else 0
        if initial_cost > f:
            continue
        heapq.heappush(pq, (initial_cost, x, 0, o))
    
    # Dictionary to store minimum cost for each state (x, y, oxygen)
    visited = {}
    min_cost = float('inf')
    
    while pq:
        cost, x, y, oxygen = heapq.heappop(pq)
        
        # If we reached the bottom row (y = H-1), update minimum cost
        if y == H - 1:
            min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
        
        # Skip if we have a better cost for this state
        state = (x, y, oxygen)
        if state in visited and visited[state] <= cost:
            continue
        visited[state] = cost
        
        # Try all possible moves: left, right, down
        for dx, dy in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
            nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
            
            # Check bounds
            if ny >= H or nx < 0 or nx >= W:
                continue
                
            # We can only move down, left, right (no up)
            # But we're already generating only valid directions
            
            # Check if we have enough oxygen to make this move
            if oxygen <= 0:
                continue
                
            # Moving to (nx, ny) consumes 1 oxygen
            new_oxygen = oxygen - 1
            new_cost = cost
            
            # Get the cell value in (nx, ny)
            cell_value = grid[ny][nx]
            
            # If it's soil (negative value), we must pay the digging cost
            if cell_value < 0:
                new_cost += abs(cell_value)
                # If new cost exceeds max excavation cost, skip
                if new_cost > f:
                    continue
            
            # If it's an oxygen cell (positive value), we replenish when we reach it
            if cell_value > 0:
                # When we reach this cell, we replenish our oxygen
                new_oxygen = min(new_oxygen + cell_value, m)
            
            # Create new state
            new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen)
            
            # If we haven't seen this state or have a better cost, add to queue
            if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
```

```python
import heapq

while True:
    line = input().strip()
    if line == "0 0":
        break
        
    W, H = map(int, line.split())
    f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
    
    grid = []
    for _ in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        grid.append(row)
    
    # Dijkstra: (cost, x, y, oxygen)
    pq = []
    
    # Start from all cells in top row (y=0)
    for x in range(W):
        # We start by digging cell (x,0), so we pay cost for that cell
        initial_cost = abs(grid[0][x]) if grid[0][x] < 0 else 0
        if initial_cost > f:
            continue
        heapq.heappush(pq, (initial_cost, x, 0, o))
    
    # Dictionary to store minimum cost for each state (x, y, oxygen)
    visited = {}
    min_cost = float('inf')
    
    while pq:
        cost, x, y, oxygen = heapq.heappop(pq)
        
        # If we reached the bottom row (y = H-1), update minimum cost
        if y == H - 1:
            min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
        
        # Skip if we have a better cost for this state
        state = (x, y, oxygen)
        if state in visited and visited[state] <= cost:
            continue
        visited[state] = cost
        
        # Try all possible moves: left, right, down
        for dx, dy in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
            nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
            
            # Check bounds
            if ny >= H or nx < 0 or nx >= W:
                continue
                
            # We can only move down, left, right (no up)
            # But we're already generating only valid directions
            
            # Check if we have enough oxygen to make this move
            if oxygen <= 0:
                continue
                
            # Moving to (nx, ny) consumes 1 oxygen
            new_oxygen = oxygen - 1
            new_cost = cost
            
            # Get the cell value in (nx, ny)
            cell_value = grid[ny][nx]
            
            # If it's soil (negative value), we must pay the digging cost
            if cell_value < 0:
                new_cost += abs(cell_value)
                # If new cost exceeds max excavation cost, skip
                if new_cost > f:
                    continue
            
            # If it's an oxygen cell (positive value), we replenish when we reach it
            if cell_value > 0:
                # When we reach this cell, we replenish our oxygen
                new_oxygen = min(new_oxygen + cell_value, m)
            
            # Create new state
            new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen)
            
            # If we haven't seen this state or have a better cost, add to queue
            if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
                heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen))
    
    if min_cost == float('inf'):
        print("NA")
    else:
        print(min_cost)
```

View Extracted Code 
import heapq

while True:
    line = input().strip()
    if line == "0 0":
        break
        
    W, H = map(int, line.split())
    f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
    
    grid = []
    for _ in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        grid.append(row)
    
    # Dijkstra: (cost, x, y, oxygen)
    pq = []
    
    # Start from all cells in top row (y=0)
    for x in range(W):
        # We start by digging cell (x,0), so we pay cost for that cell
        initial_cost = abs(grid[0][x]) if grid[0][x] < 0 else 0
        if initial_cost > f:
            continue
        heapq.heappush(pq, (initial_cost, x, 0, o))
    
    # Dictionary to store minimum cost for each state (x, y, oxygen)
    visited = {}
    min_cost = float('inf')
    
    while pq:
        cost, x, y, oxygen = heapq.heappop(pq)
        
        # If we reached the bottom row (y = H-1), update minimum cost
        if y == H - 1:
            min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
        
        # Skip if we have a better cost for this state
        state = (x, y, oxygen)
        if state in visited and visited[state] <= cost:
            continue
        visited[state] = cost
        
        # Try all possible moves: left, right, down
        for dx, dy in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
            nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
            
            # Check bounds
            if ny >= H or nx < 0 or nx >= W:
                continue
                
            # We can only move down, left, right (no up)
            # But we're already generating only valid directions
            
            # Check if we have enough oxygen to make this move
            if oxygen <= 0:
                continue
                
            # Moving to (nx, ny) consumes 1 oxygen
            new_oxygen = oxygen - 1
            new_cost = cost
            
            # Get the cell value in (nx, ny)
            cell_value = grid[ny][nx]
            
            # If it's soil (negative value), we must pay the digging cost
            if cell_value < 0:
                new_cost += abs(cell_value)
                # If new cost exceeds max excavation cost, skip
                if new_cost > f:
                    continue
            
            # If it's an oxygen cell (positive value), we replenish when we reach it
            if cell_value > 0:
                # When we reach this cell, we replenish our oxygen
                new_oxygen = min(new_oxygen + cell_value, m)
            
            # Create new state
            new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen)
            
            # If we haven't seen this state or have a better cost, add to queue
            if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
                heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen))
    
    if min_cost == float('inf'):
        print("NA")
    else:
        print(min_cost)

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Aizu has an ancient legend of buried treasure. You have finally found the place where the buried treasure is buried. Since we know the depth of the buried treasure and the condition of the strata to be dug, we can reach the buried treasure at the lowest cost with careful planning. So you decided to create a program that reads the condition of the formation and calculates the route to reach the buried treasure depth at the lowest cost.

The state of the formation is represented by cells arranged in a two-dimensional grid, and the position of each cell is represented by the coordinates (x, y). Let the upper left be (1,1), and assume that the x coordinate increases as it goes to the right and the y coordinate increases as it goes deeper down. You choose one of the cells with the smallest y-coordinate and start digging from there, then dig into one of the cells with the largest y-coordinate. There are two types of cells in the formation:

1. A cell filled with soil. There is a fixed cost for each cell to dig.
2. Oxygen-filled cell. There is no need to dig, and each cell can be replenished with a fixed amount of oxygen. The oxygen in the cell that has been replenished with oxygen is exhausted and cannot be replenished again. Also, when you reach this cell, you must replenish oxygen.



Only left, right, and down cells can be dug from a cell. Once you have dug a cell, you can move it left or right, but you cannot move it up.

You must carry an oxygen cylinder with you when excavating. The moment the oxygen cylinder reaches zero, you will not be able to move, excavate, or replenish oxygen. The remaining amount is decremented by 1 each time you move the cell. Even if the remaining amount of the oxygen cylinder is 0 and the depth of the buried treasure is reached, it is not considered to have been reached. In addition, oxygen can be replenished in cells that have accumulated oxygen, but the excess capacity is discarded.

Create a program that inputs the size of the formation, the excavation cost, the capacity of the oxygen cylinder, the amount of oxygen in the initial state, and the information of the formation, and outputs the minimum cost to reach the deepest cell. However, if the minimum cost exceeds the excavation cost, or if you cannot reach the buried treasure no matter how you dig, please output "NA".

The second line is the integer f (1 ≤ f ≤ 10000) that represents your excavation cost, the integer m (3 ≤ m ≤ 50) that represents the capacity of the oxygen cylinder, and the integer o that represents the amount of oxygen you have in the initial state. o ≤ m) is given.

The following H line is given the geological information ci, j. ci, j represents the cell information for coordinates (i, j) and is given in the following format:
If the value is negative, the cell is full of soil and the value represents the cost.
If the value is positive, it is a cell filled with oxygen, and the value represents the amount of oxygen.
However, there are no more than 50 cells in which oxygen has accumulated.

The number of datasets does not exceed 50.

Print the minimum cost or NA on one line for each dataset.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a 2D grid where we start at the cell with smallest y-coordinate (top row)
2. We can only move left, right, or down (no up movement)
3. Cells can be:
   - Soil: have a digging cost (negative value)
   - Oxygen: have oxygen capacity (positive value), can replenish oxygen when visited
4. We carry an oxygen cylinder with initial amount `o`
5. Oxygen decreases by 1 for each move (including when moving to a new cell)
6. When reaching an oxygen cell, we must replenish oxygen (the oxygen from that cell is consumed)
7. We need to reach the deepest cell (largest y-coordinate, which is the last row)
8. We want minimum total excavation cost to reach the deepest cell
9. If cost exceeds excavation cost `f` or we can't reach the deepest cell, output "NA"

## Key Observations

1. We start at the top row (y=1) and need to reach the bottom row (y=H)
2. We can only move right, left, or down
3. Oxygen is consumed with each move, and we can replenish at oxygen cells
4. We need to find the path with minimum cost that reaches the bottom row
5. This is a shortest path problem with state (row, col, oxygen_remaining)

## Algorithm Approach

I'll use BFS or Dijkstra's algorithm where each state is (row, col, oxygen_remaining) and we track the minimum cost to reach each state.

Steps:
1. Parse input
2. For each starting position in top row (since we start at smallest y-coordinate)
3. Use state space search with state (row, col, oxygen_remaining)
4. From each state, try moving to adjacent cells (left, right, down)
5. Track minimum cost to reach any cell in the last row
6. Check if the cost exceeds f or if we can't reach the bottom row

## Implementation

```python
from collections import defaultdict
import heapq
import sys

def solve():
    while True:
        line = input().split()
        W, H = int(line[0]), int(line[1])
        if W == 0 and H == 0:
            break
            
        f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
        
        grid = []
        for _ in range(H):
            row = list(map(int, input().split()))
            grid.append(row)
        
        # If we can't reach bottom row, or cost exceeds f, return NA
        # We need to find minimum cost path from any top row cell to any bottom row cell
        
        # State: (cost, row, col, oxygen_remaining)
        # Use Dijkstra's algorithm
        pq = []
        visited = set()
        
        # Initialize with all starting positions in top row (row 0, since 1-indexed in problem)
        # But grid is 0-indexed, so top row is row 0
        for col in range(W):
            # Start at (0, col) with initial oxygen o
            if grid[0][col] < 0:
                # Soil cell, we need to pay excavation cost
                start_cost = grid[0][col]
            else:
                # Oxygen cell, but we start with initial oxygen o
                start_cost = 0
                
            # Add to priority queue
            heapq.heappush(pq, (start_cost, 0, col, o))
            visited.add((0, col, o))
        
        min_cost = float('inf')
        
        while pq:
            cost, row, col, oxygen_remaining = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            # If we reached the bottom row, update min_cost
            if row == H - 1:
                min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
                continue
            
            # Try all valid moves: left, right, down
            for dr, dc in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
                new_row = row + dr
                new_col = col + dc
                
                # Check bounds
                if new_row < 0 or new_row >= H or new_col < 0 or new_col >= W:
                    continue
                
                # Check if we already visited this state with lower or equal cost
                state = (new_row, new_col, oxygen_remaining)
                if state in visited:
                    continue
                    
                # Check if we can make this move (oxygen must be > 0 after move)
                if oxygen_remaining <= 0:
                    # Cannot move if oxygen is 0
                    continue
                
                # Move consumes 1 oxygen
                new_oxygen = oxygen_remaining - 1

                cell_val = grid[new_row][new_col]

                    new_cost += cell_val

                    # But we consume 1 oxygen for moving

                    if new_oxygen < 0:  # This shouldn't happen due to previous check
                        continue

                    # Actually, when we reach an oxygen cell, we must replenish
                    # The replenishment happens upon reaching the cell
                    # So we add the oxygen amount from this cell
                    additional_oxygen = cell_val
                    new_oxygen += additional_oxygen
                    # Cap at capacity
                    new_oxygen = min(new_oxygen, m)
                    
                    # New cost remains same (no excavation cost)
                    new_cost = cost
                # But wait - I might be misunderstanding the replenishment timing
                    
                # Let me re-read: "when you reach this cell, you must replenish oxygen"

                if grid[new_row][new_col] > 0:
```

The issue is in how I'm handling the oxygen replenishment and cost calculation. Let me re-read:

- When you reach an oxygen cell, you must replenish oxygen (the oxygen in that cell is exhausted and cannot be replenished again)
- Oxygen is consumed by each move (decreased by 1)

So the sequence for a move to a cell:
1. Move to the cell - oxygen decreases by 1 (so must have at least 1 oxygen)
2. When you arrive at a cell:
   - If it's soil: pay excavation cost (negative value)
   - If it's oxygen: replenish with the amount of oxygen (add to your cylinder, capped at m)

```python
from collections import defaultdict
import heapq

def solve():
    while True:
        line = input().split()
        W, H = int(line[0]), int(line[1])
        if W == 0 and H == 0:
            break
            
        f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
        
        grid = []
        for _ in range(H):
            row = list(map(int, input().split()))
            grid.append(row)
        
        # Dijkstra's algorithm with state (cost, row, col, oxygen_remaining)
        pq = []
        visited = set()
        
        # Start from all cells in the top row (row 0)
        for col in range(W):
            # Start at (0, col) with initial oxygen o
            # The starting cell might be soil or oxygen
            start_cost = 0
            if grid[0][col] < 0:
                start_cost = grid[0][col]
            else:
                # Oxygen cell - we start with initial oxygen o
                start_cost = 0
                
            heapq.heappush(pq, (start_cost, 0, col, o))
            visited.add((0, col, o))
        
        min_cost = float('inf')
        
        while pq:
            cost, row, col, oxygen_remaining = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            # If we reached the bottom row, update min_cost
            if row == H - 1:
                min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
                continue
            
            # Try all moves: left, right, down
            for dr, dc in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
                new_row = row + dr
                new_col = col + dc
                
                # Check bounds
                if new_row < 0 or new_row >= H or new_col < 0 or new_col >= W:
                    continue
                
                # Check if we have enough oxygen to make this move
                if oxygen_remaining <= 0:
                    continue
                    
                # Move consumes 1 oxygen
                new_oxygen_after_move = oxygen_remaining - 1
                
                # Process the new cell
                new_cell_value = grid[new_row][new_col]
                if new_cell_value > 0:
                    # Oxygen cell - we must replenish with this oxygen
                    new_oxygen_after_replenish = new_oxygen_after_move + new_cell_value
                    new_oxygen_after_replenish = min(new_oxygen_after_replenish, m)
                else:
                    # Soil cell - no replenishment
                    new_oxygen_after_replenish = new_oxygen_after_move
                
                # Check if new oxygen is valid
                if new_oxygen_after_replenish < 0:
                    continue
                    
                # Calculate new cost
                new_cost = cost
                if new_cell_value < 0:
                    new_cost += new_cell_value
                
                # Create state
                state = (new_row, new_col, new_oxygen_after_replenish)
                if state not in visited:
                    visited.add(state)
                    heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, new_row, new_col, new_oxygen_after_replenish))
        
        # Check if we found a path
        if min_cost == float('inf'):
            print("NA")
        else:
            # Check if cost exceeds excavation cost
            if min_cost > f:
                print("NA")
            else:
                print(min_cost)
```

```python
from collections import defaultdict
import heapq

def solve():
    while True:
        line = input().split()
        W, H = int(line[0]), int(line[1])
        if W == 0 and H == 0:
            break
            
        f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
        
        grid = []
        for _ in range(H):
            row = list(map(int, input().split()))
            grid.append(row)
        
        # Dijkstra's algorithm with state (cost, row, col, oxygen_remaining)
        pq = []
        visited = set()
        
        # Start from all cells in the top row (row 0)
        for col in range(W):
            # Start at (0, col) with initial oxygen o
            start_cell_value = grid[0][col]
            start_cost = 0
            if start_cell_value < 0:
                # Soil cell - cost is the absolute value
                start_cost = -start_cell_value
            else:
                # Oxygen cell - no cost, but we will replenish when we reach it
                start_cost = 0
            
            # When we start at this cell, if it's oxygen, we replenish with its oxygen
            # So initial oxygen is o, plus the oxygen from this cell (if it's oxygen)
            # But we already have initial oxygen o. When we "reach" this cell, we replenish.
            
            # So for a starting oxygen cell, total oxygen becomes o + cell_value, capped at m
            # For a soil cell, oxygen remains o
            
            # Add to queue
            initial_oxygen = o
            if start_cell_value > 0:
                initial_oxygen += start_cell_value
                initial_oxygen = min(initial_oxygen, m)
            
            heapq.heappush(pq, (start_cost, 0, col, initial_oxygen))
            visited.add((0, col, initial_oxygen))
        
        min_cost = float('inf')
        
        while pq:
            cost, row, col, oxygen_remaining = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            # If we reached the bottom row, update min_cost
            if row == H - 1:
                min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
                continue
            
            # Try all moves: left, right, down
            for dr, dc in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
                new_row = row + dr
                new_col = col + dc
                
                # Check bounds
                if new_row < 0 or new_row >= H or new_col < 0 or new_col >= W:
                    continue
                
                # Check if we have enough oxygen to make this move
                if oxygen_remaining <= 0:
                    continue
                    
                # Move consumes 1 oxygen
                new_oxygen_after_move = oxygen_remaining - 1
                
                # Process the new cell
                new_cell_value = grid[new_row][new_col]
                if new_cell_value > 0:
                    # Oxygen cell - we must replenish with this oxygen
                    new_oxygen_after_replenish = new_oxygen_after_move + new_cell_value
                    new_oxygen_after_replenish = min(new_oxygen_after_replenish, m)
                else:
                    # Soil cell - no replenishment
                    new_oxygen_after_replenish = new_oxygen_after_move
                
                # Check if new oxygen is valid (non-negative)
                if new_oxygen_after_replenish < 0:
                    continue
                    
                # Calculate new cost
                new_cost = cost
                if new_cell_value < 0:
                    new_cost += -new_cell_value  # cost is absolute value
                
                # Create state
                state = (new_row, new_col, new_oxygen_after_replenish)
                if state not in visited:
                    visited.add(state)
                    heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, new_row, new_col, new_oxygen_after_replenish))
        
        # Check if we found a path
        if min_cost == float('inf'):
            print("NA")
        else:
            # Check if cost exceeds excavation cost
            if min_cost > f:
                print("NA")
            else:
                print(min_cost)

solve()
```

```python
import heapq

def solve():
    while True:
        line = input().split()
        W, H = int(line[0]), int(line[1])
        if W == 0 and H == 0:
            break
            
        f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
        
        grid = []
        for _ in range(H):
            row = list(map(int, input().split()))
            grid.append(row)
        
        # Dijkstra's algorithm
        pq = []  # (cost, row, col, oxygen_remaining)
        visited = set()
        
        # Start from all cells in the top row (row 0)
        for col in range(W):
            cell_value = grid[0][col]
            # Calculate cost for starting cell
            start_cost = 0
            if cell_value < 0:
                start_cost = -cell_value  # absolute value of cost
            else:
                start_cost = 0
            
            # When we start at this cell, if it's oxygen, we replenish with its oxygen
            initial_oxygen = o
            if cell_value > 0:
                initial_oxygen += cell_value
                initial_oxygen = min(initial_oxygen, m)
            else:
                initial_oxygen = o  # remains o for soil cells
            
            heapq.heappush(pq, (start_cost, 0, col, initial_oxygen))
            visited.add((0, col, initial_oxygen))
        
        min_cost = float('inf')
        
        while pq:
            cost, row, col, oxygen_remaining = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            # If we reached the bottom row, update min_cost
            if row == H - 1:
                min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
                continue
            
            # Try all moves: left, right, down
            for dr, dc in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
                new_row = row + dr
                new_col = col + dc
                
                # Check bounds
                if new_row < 0 or new_row >= H or new_col < 0 or new_col >= W:
                    continue
                
                # Can only move if we have oxygen
                if oxygen_remaining <= 0:
                    continue
                    
                # Move consumes 1 oxygen
                oxygen_after_move = oxygen_remaining - 1
                
                # Process the new cell
                new_cell_value = grid[new_row][new_col]
                if new_cell_value > 0:
                    # Oxygen cell - replenish with that oxygen
                    oxygen_after_replenish = oxygen_after_move + new_cell_value
                    oxygen_after_replenish = min(oxygen_after_replenish, m)
                else:
                    # Soil cell - no replenishment
                    oxygen_after_replenish = oxygen_after_move
                
                # Must have non-negative oxygen
                if oxygen_after_replenish < 0:
                    continue
                    
                # Calculate new cost
                new_cost = cost
                if new_cell_value < 0:
                    new_cost += -new_cell_value  # absolute value for soil cost
                
                # Create state
                state = (new_row, new_col, oxygen_after_replenish)
                if state not in visited:
                    visited.add(state)
                    heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, new_row, new_col, oxygen_after_replenish))
        
        if min_cost == float('inf'):
            print("NA")
        elif min_cost > f:
            print("NA")
        else:
            print(min_cost)

solve()
```

        line = input().split()

        visited = set()

        for col in range(W):

            # Calculate cost for starting cell
            start_cost = 0
            if cell_value < 0:
                start_cost = -cell_value  # absolute value of cost
            else:
                start_cost = 0
            
            # When we start at this cell, if it's oxygen, we replenish with its oxygen
            initial_oxygen = o
            if cell_value > 0:
                initial_oxygen += cell_value
                initial_oxygen = min(initial_oxygen, m)
            else:
                initial_oxygen = o  # remains o for soil cells
            
            heapq.heappush(pq, (start_cost, 0, col, initial_oxygen))
            visited.add((0, col, initial_oxygen))
        
        min_cost = float('inf')
        
        while pq:
            cost, row, col, oxygen_remaining = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            # If we reached the bottom row, update min_cost
            if row == H - 1:
                min_cost = min(min_cost, cost)
                continue
            
            # Try all moves: left, right, down
            for dr, dc in [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]:
                new_row = row + dr

                    continue

                if oxygen_remaining <= 0:
                    continue

                    oxygen_after_replenish = oxygen_after_move

                    visited.add(state)

        if min_cost == float('inf'):
            print("NA")
        elif min_cost > f:
            print("NA")
        else:
            print(min_cost)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Aizu has an ancient legend of buried treasure. You have finally found the place where the buried treasure is buried. Since we know the depth of the buried treasure and the condition of the strata to be dug, we can reach the buried treasure at the lowest cost with careful planning. So you decided to create a program that reads the condition of the formation and calculates the route to reach the buried treasure depth at the lowest cost.

The state of the formation is represented by cells arranged in a two-dimensional grid, and the position of each cell is represented by the coordinates (x, y). Let the upper left be (1,1), and assume that the x coordinate increases as it goes to the right and the y coordinate increases as it goes deeper down. You choose one of the cells with the smallest y-coordinate and start digging from there, then dig into one of the cells with the largest y-coordinate. There are two types of cells in the formation:

1. A cell filled with soil. There is a fixed cost for each cell to dig.
2. Oxygen-filled cell. There is no need to dig, and each cell can be replenished with a fixed amount of oxygen. The oxygen in the cell that has been replenished with oxygen is exhausted and cannot be replenished again. Also, when you reach this cell, you must replenish oxygen.



Only left, right, and down cells can be dug from a cell. Once you have dug a cell, you can move it left or right, but you cannot move it up.

You must carry an oxygen cylinder with you when excavating. The moment the oxygen cylinder reaches zero, you will not be able to move, excavate, or replenish oxygen. The remaining amount is decremented by 1 each time you move the cell. Even if the remaining amount of the oxygen cylinder is 0 and the depth of the buried treasure is reached, it is not considered to have been reached. In addition, oxygen can be replenished in cells that have accumulated oxygen, but the excess capacity is discarded.

Create a program that inputs the size of the formation, the excavation cost, the capacity of the oxygen cylinder, the amount of oxygen in the initial state, and the information of the formation, and outputs the minimum cost to reach the deepest cell. However, if the minimum cost exceeds the excavation cost, or if you cannot reach the buried treasure no matter how you dig, please output "NA".

<image>



Input

A sequence of multiple datasets is given as input. The end of the input is indicated by two zero lines. Each dataset is given in the following format.


W H
f m o
c1,1 c2,1 ... cW,1
c1,2 c2,2 ... cW,2
...
c1, H c2, H ... cW, H


The horizontal size W of the formation and the vertical size H (3 ≤ W, H ≤ 10) are given in the first line. The second line is the integer f (1 ≤ f ≤ 10000) that represents your excavation cost, the integer m (3 ≤ m ≤ 50) that represents the capacity of the oxygen cylinder, and the integer o that represents the amount of oxygen you have in the initial state. o ≤ m) is given.

The following H line is given the geological information ci, j. ci, j represents the cell information for coordinates (i, j) and is given in the following format:
If the value is negative, the cell is full of soil and the value represents the cost.
If the value is positive, it is a cell filled with oxygen, and the value represents the amount of oxygen.
However, there are no more than 50 cells in which oxygen has accumulated.

The number of datasets does not exceed 50.

Output

Print the minimum cost or NA on one line for each dataset.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a 2D grid of cells with coordinates (x,y) where (1,1) is top-left and y increases downward
2. We start at the cell with smallest y-coordinate (top row)
3. We must reach the cell with largest y-coordinate (bottom row) - this is our target
4. We can only move: left, right, or down (not up)
5. Two types of cells:
   - Soil cells: have excavation cost (negative value)
   - Oxygen cells: have oxygen amount (positive value)
6. Oxygen cylinder capacity is `m`, initial oxygen is `o`
7. Oxygen is consumed by 1 unit per move (including moving to/from oxygen cells)
8. When we reach an oxygen cell, we must replenish oxygen (the amount is the value of the cell, but we can't exceed capacity)
9. We need to find the minimum excavation cost to reach the deepest level (bottom row)
10. If minimum cost exceeds excavation cost f, or if impossible to reach, output "NA"

## Key Observations

1. We start at the top row (y=1) and need to reach any cell in the bottom row (y=H)
2. We can only move left, right, down
3. Oxygen is consumed by each move, so we need to plan our path carefully
4. We can only replenish oxygen when we reach an oxygen cell
5. We must carry oxygen cylinder with us at all times
6. We cannot go up from any cell
7. The problem is essentially finding the shortest path from any top row cell to any bottom row cell, considering both excavation cost and oxygen constraints

## Solution Approach

This is a shortest path problem with state space including:
- Current position (x,y)
- Current oxygen level

Since the grid is small (W, H ≤ 10), we can use BFS or Dijkstra with state (x, y, oxygen_level).

Steps:
1. Parse input
2. For each possible start position in row 1 (top row)
3. Use Dijkstra or BFS with state (x, y, oxygen) to find minimum cost to reach any cell in last row
4. For each state, try moving to adjacent cells (left, right, down)
5. Track oxygen consumption and replenishment
6. Return minimum cost if it's within excavation budget, otherwise "NA"

## Implementation

```python
import heapq
from collections import defaultdict
import sys

def solve_dataset():
    line = input().strip()
    while not line:
        line = input().strip()
    
    W, H = map(int, line.split())
    
    if W == 0 and H == 0:
        return None
    
    line = input().strip()
    f, m, o = map(int, line.split())
    
    grid = []
    for _ in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        grid.append(row)
    
    # If we can't move at all (no valid path), return NA
    # We need to check if there's any path from top row to bottom row
    
    # Dijkstra-like search with state (x, y, oxygen)
    # We want to reach any cell in the last row
    # Start from any cell in first row (y=0 in 0-indexed)
    
    # Priority queue: (cost, x, y, oxygen)
    pq = []
    
    # Initialize: start from each cell in first row
    for x in range(W):
        # Start with initial oxygen and cost 0
        initial_oxygen = o
        heapq.heappush(pq, (0, x, 0, initial_oxygen))
    
    # We'll track visited states to avoid cycles
    # state: (x, y, oxygen) -> min_cost
    visited = {}
    
    # Directions: left, right, down
    # Note: we can't go up
    directions = [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]  # left, right, down
    
    min_cost_to_target = float('inf')
    
    while pq:
        cost, x, y, oxygen = heapq.heappop(pq)
        
        # If we've reached the bottom row, record the cost
        if y == H - 1:
            min_cost_to_target = min(min_cost_to_target, cost)
            continue
        
        # Skip if we've seen this state with lower cost
        state = (x, y, oxygen)
        if state in visited and visited[state] <= cost:
            continue
        visited[state] = cost
        
        # Try all three directions
        for dx, dy in directions:
            nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
            
            # Check bounds
            if nx < 0 or nx >= W or ny < 0 or ny >= H:
                continue
                
            # Calculate oxygen after move
            new_oxygen = oxygen - 1  # oxygen consumed by movement
            
            # If oxygen becomes negative, this path is invalid
            if new_oxygen < 0:
                continue
                
            # If we move to a cell with oxygen, we must replenish
            cell_val = grid[ny][nx]
            
            # If it's an oxygen cell (positive value)
            if cell_val > 0:
                # Replenish oxygen - but can't exceed capacity
                new_oxygen = min(new_oxygen + cell_val, m)
            
            # If it's a soil cell (negative value), add excavation cost
            # But we can only come here if we have enough oxygen
            new_cost = cost
            if cell_val < 0:
                new_cost += cell_val
            
            # Add to queue with new state
            new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen)
            if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
                heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen))
    
    # Check if we found a valid path
    if min_cost_to_target == float('inf'):
        return "NA"
    
    # Check if minimum cost exceeds excavation cost
    if min_cost_to_target > f:
        return "NA"
    
    return str(min_cost_to_target)

# Main loop
results = []
while True:
    result = solve_dataset()
    if result is None:
        break
    results.append(result)

for res in results:
    print(res)
```

Wait, I need to be more careful about the oxygen replenishment and movement. Let me reconsider the logic.

The key issue is: when we reach an oxygen cell, we must replenish oxygen. But the oxygen consumption happens during movement, not when we arrive at the cell.

Let me fix this:

```python
import heapq
from collections import defaultdict

def solve_dataset():
    line = input().strip()
    while not line:
        line = input().strip()
    
    W, H = map(int, line.split())
    
    if W == 0 and H == 0:
        return None
    
    line = input().strip()
    f, m, o = map(int, line.split())
    
    grid = []
    for _ in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        grid.append(row)
    
    # Dijkstra with state (x, y, oxygen)
    pq = []
    
    # Start from each cell in first row (y=0 in 0-indexed)
    for x in range(W):
        heapq.heappush(pq, (0, x, 0, o))  # (cost, x, y, oxygen)
    
    # State: (x, y, oxygen) -> min_cost
    visited = {}
    
    # Directions: left, right, down
    directions = [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]
    
    min_cost_to_target = float('inf')
    
    while pq:
        cost, x, y, oxygen = heapq.heappop(pq)
        
        # If reached bottom row, update minimum cost
        if y == H - 1:
            min_cost_to_target = min(min_cost_to_target, cost)
            continue
        
        # Skip if we've seen this state with better cost
        state = (x, y, oxygen)
        if state in visited and visited[state] <= cost:
            continue
        visited[state] = cost
        
        # Try moving to adjacent cells
        for dx, dy in directions:
            nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
            
            # Check bounds
            if nx < 0 or nx >= W or ny < 0 or ny >= H:
                continue
                
            # Movement consumes 1 oxygen
            new_oxygen = oxygen - 1
            
            # If oxygen goes negative, this move is invalid
            if new_oxygen < 0:
                continue
                
            # After moving into the new cell, check what type of cell we're in
            cell_val = grid[ny][nx]
            
            # If it's an oxygen cell, replenish oxygen (up to capacity)
            if cell_val > 0:
                new_oxygen = min(new_oxygen + cell_val, m)
            
            # If it's a soil cell, add excavation cost
            new_cost = cost
            if cell_val < 0:
                new_cost += cell_val
            
            # If we have a better cost for this state, add to queue
            new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen)
            if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
                heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen))
    
    # Check if any path found and cost within budget
    if min_cost_to_target == float('inf'):
        return "NA"
    
    if min_cost_to_target > f:
        return "NA"
    
    return str(min_cost_to_target)

# Main loop
while True:
    result = solve_dataset()
    if result is None:
        break
    print(result)
```

import heapq
from collections import defaultdict

def solve_dataset():
    line = input().strip()
    while not line:
        line = input().strip()
    
    W, H = map(int, line.split())
    
    if W == 0 and H == 0:
        return None
    
    line = input().strip()
    f, m, o = map(int, line.split())
    
    grid = []
    for _ in range(H):
        row = list(map(int, input().split()))
        grid.append(row)
    
    # Dijkstra with state (x, y, oxygen)
    pq = []
    
    # Start from each cell in first row (y=0 in 0-indexed)
    for x in range(W):
        heapq.heappush(pq, (0, x, 0, o))  # (cost, x, y, oxygen)
    
    # State: (x, y, oxygen) -> min_cost
    visited = {}
    
    # Directions: left, right, down
    directions = [(0, -1), (0, 1), (1, 0)]
    
    min_cost_to_target = float('inf')
    
    while pq:
        cost, x, y, oxygen = heapq.heappop(pq)
        
        # If reached bottom row, update minimum cost
        if y == H - 1:
            min_cost_to_target = min(min_cost_to_target, cost)
            continue
        
        # Skip if we've seen this state with better cost
        state = (x, y, oxygen)
        if state in visited and visited[state] <= cost:
            continue
        visited[state] = cost
        
        # Try moving to adjacent cells
        for dx, dy in directions:
            nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
            
            # Check bounds
            if nx < 0 or nx >= W or ny < 0 or ny >= H:
                continue
                
            # Movement consumes 1 oxygen
            new_oxygen = oxygen - 1
            
            # If oxygen goes negative, this move is invalid
            if new_oxygen < 0:
                continue
                
            # After moving into the new cell, check what type of cell we're in
            cell_val = grid[ny][nx]
            
            # If it's an oxygen cell, replenish oxygen (up to capacity)
            if cell_val > 0:
                new_oxygen = min(new_oxygen + cell_val, m)
            
            # If it's a soil cell, add excavation cost
            new_cost = cost
            if cell_val < 0:
                new_cost += cell_val
            
            # If we have a better cost for this state, add to queue
            new_state = (nx, ny, new_oxygen)
            if new_state not in visited or visited[new_state] > new_cost:
                heapq.heappush(pq, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen))
    
    # Check if any path found and cost within budget
    if min_cost_to_target == float('inf'):
        return "NA"
    
    if min_cost_to_target > f:
        return "NA"
    
    return str(min_cost_to_target)

# Main loop
while True:
    result = solve_dataset()
    if result is None:
        break
    print(result)

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Aizu has an ancient legend of buried treasure. You have finally found the place where the buried treasure is buried. Since we know the depth of the buried treasure and the condition of the strata to be dug, we can reach the buried treasure at the lowest cost with careful planning. So you decided to create a program that reads the condition of the formation and calculates the route to reach the buried treasure depth at the lowest cost.

The state of the formation is represented by cells arranged in a two-dimensional grid, and the position of each cell is represented by the coordinates (x, y). Let the upper left be (1,1), and assume that the x coordinate increases as it goes to the right and the y coordinate increases as it goes deeper down. You choose one of the cells with the smallest y-coordinate and start digging from there, then dig into one of the cells with the largest y-coordinate. There are two types of cells in the formation:

1. A cell filled with soil. There is a fixed cost for each cell to dig.
2. Oxygen-filled cell. There is no need to dig, and each cell can be replenished with a fixed amount of oxygen. The oxygen in the cell that has been replenished with oxygen is exhausted and cannot be replenished again. Also, when you reach this cell, you must replenish oxygen.



Only left, right, and down cells can be dug from a cell. Once you have dug a cell, you can move it left or right, but you cannot move it up.

You must carry an oxygen cylinder with you when excavating. The moment the oxygen cylinder reaches zero, you will not be able to move, excavate, or replenish oxygen. The remaining amount is decremented by 1 each time you move the cell. Even if the remaining amount of the oxygen cylinder is 0 and the depth of the buried treasure is reached, it is not considered to have been reached. In addition, oxygen can be replenished in cells that have accumulated oxygen, but the excess capacity is discarded.

Create a program that inputs the size of the formation, the excavation cost, the capacity of the oxygen cylinder, the amount of oxygen in the initial state, and the information of the formation, and outputs the minimum cost to reach the deepest cell. However, if the minimum cost exceeds the excavation cost, or if you cannot reach the buried treasure no matter how you dig, please output "NA".

<image>



Input

A sequence of multiple datasets is given as input. The end of the input is indicated by two zero lines. Each dataset is given in the following format.


W H
f m o
c1,1 c2,1 ... cW,1
c1,2 c2,2 ... cW,2
...
c1, H c2, H ... cW, H


The horizontal size W of the formation and the vertical size H (3 ≤ W, H ≤ 10) are given in the first line. The second line is the integer f (1 ≤ f ≤ 10000) that represents your excavation cost, the integer m (3 ≤ m ≤ 50) that represents the capacity of the oxygen cylinder, and the integer o that represents the amount of oxygen you have in the initial state. o ≤ m) is given.

The following H line is given the geological information ci, j. ci, j represents the cell information for coordinates (i, j) and is given in the following format:
If the value is negative, the cell is full of soil and the value represents the cost.
If the value is positive, it is a cell filled with oxygen, and the value represents the amount of oxygen.
However, there are no more than 50 cells in which oxygen has accumulated.

The number of datasets does not exceed 50.

Output

Print the minimum cost or NA on one line for each dataset.

Otherwise, after exploring all, if no path to bottom row, return "NA".

Also, if at any point the cost exceeds f, we can skip, but in Dijkstra, we might have high cost states that are not optimal, so we just let it run and if a state has cost > f, we can skip adding it to queue? But since we're using min-heap, and cost is non-negative, we might have to consider it.

Actually, digging cost is positive, so total cost is non-decreasing.

But if a state has cost already > f, we can skip it, because we can't exceed f.

So we can add a check: if current_cost > f, skip.

Similarly, when we create new state, if new_cost > f, we skip adding to queue.

That helps prune.

Also, when we reach bottom row, we check if cost <= f, and if so, we output it.

But we want the minimum cost among all paths to bottom row.

In Dijkstra, when we first reach a state in the bottom row, it might not be the minimum cost if there's a cheaper path later? No, Dijkstra finds shortest path in terms of cost.

Since all edge costs are non-negative (digging cost is positive, oxygen is free), Dijkstra will find the minimum cost.

But oxygen is free, and digging cost is positive, so yes, non-negative.

So when we first reach a cell in bottom row, that's the minimum cost.

But we might reach it multiple times with different oxygen levels and used sets, but we want the overall minimum cost.

So when we pop a state and y == H-1, we should return the cost immediately? Not necessarily, because there might be a cheaper path to bottom row with a different state.

Actually, in Dijkstra, when we first visit a node (x,y) with a certain state, the cost is the minimum for that state. But we are storing state with (x,y,oxygen_level,used_set), so different states are different.

And when we pop a state with y=H-1, that's a solution.

But there might be multiple solutions to the bottom row with different costs.

We want the minimum cost among all solutions.

So we should keep track of the minimum cost to reach any cell in bottom row.

Actually, when we first reach a state with y=H-1, we can record the cost, but there might be a cheaper path later.

Since we use Dijkstra with min-heap, the first time we reach a state with y=H-1, it might not be the minimum cost because there might be multiple ways to reach bottom row.

But the cost is additive and non-negative, so once we reach a state in bottom row with cost c, and we haven't found a lower cost yet, then any future state in bottom row will have cost >= c, so we can stop when we find the minimum.

But we need to find the global minimum cost to reach any cell in bottom row.

So we should not return immediately on the first pop of bottom row.

Instead, we should continue until the heap is empty or we have found a path.

But that's inefficient.

Actually, since all edge costs are non-negative, the first time we reach a state in the bottom row, it is the minimum cost for that state, but there might be other states in bottom row with lower cost.

So we need to check all possible states in bottom row.

We can store the minimum cost to reach any bottom row cell with any state, and update it.

But we can't easily store that in a global variable.

Alternative: when we pop a state and y == H-1, we keep track of min_cost = min(min_cost, current_cost)

Then continue until the heap is empty.

Then at the end, if min_cost <= f, output min_cost, else "NA".

But if the heap is empty and we never found a valid path, then min_cost remains infinity.

We can do that.

Let me implement that.

Steps:

1. Initialize min_final_cost = infinity
2. Use heap to store (cost, x, y, oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set)
3. While heap not empty:
   a. Pop the smallest cost state
   b. If this state's y == H-1, update min_final_cost = min(min_final_cost, cost)
   c. If cost > f, skip (but already in heap, we can skip if cost > f, but maybe we already check when adding)
   d. If we have a better state (same (x,y,oxygen_level,used_set) with lower cost), skip (using a visited dictionary)
   e. Otherwise, for each valid neighbor (left, right, down), if oxygen_level > 0:
        - new_oxygen_level = oxygen_level - 1
        - new_cost = cost
        - if neighbor is soil cell: new_cost += -grid[i][j]
        - if neighbor is oxygen cell and neighbor not in used_oxygen_set:
            - new_oxygen_level = min(new_oxygen_level + oxygen_amount, m)
            - new_used_set = used_oxygen_set | {neighbor}
        - else if neighbor is oxygen cell and already in used_oxygen_set: 
            - new_oxygen_level = new_oxygen_level  (no change)
            - new_used_set = used_oxygen_set
        - if new_cost > f: skip
        - if (x_new, y_new, new_oxygen_level, new_used_set) not in visited_states, add to heap and visited_states
4. After while loop, if min_final_cost is still infinity, output "NA", else output min_final_cost

But we need to check if new_oxygen_level >= 0, but since we only move when oxygen_level > 0, and we subtract 1, new_oxygen_level = oxygen_level - 1 >= 0.

So it's non-negative.

Also, for oxygen level after adding, it's capped by m, so non-negative.

Now, for the state representation: we represent a state as (x, y, oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set)

We only store unique states to avoid cycles.

We can use a dictionary: visited[(x,y,oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set)] = min_cost

When we pop a state, if we have seen this state with lower cost, skip.

Otherwise, add to visited and to heap.

Now, let's code accordingly.

I need to be careful with the coordinate system.

In grid, row 0 is top, row H-1 is bottom.

So we start at any cell in row 0.

For each move: left, right, down.

Left: (x-1, y) if x-1 >= 0
Right: (x+1, y) if x+1 < W
Down: (x, y+1) if y+1 < H

Also, when we are at a cell, we have oxygen_level and used_oxygen_set.

Now, let's implement.

We also need to store the oxygen amount for each oxygen cell.

First, when reading the grid, we store grid[i][j] for i in 0..W-1, j in 0..H-1.

For each cell, if grid[i][j] > 0, it's an oxygen cell with amount = grid[i][j].

We'll create a list of oxygen cells: for each (i,j) where grid[i][j] > 0, we have amount = grid[i][j].

In the state, when we go to (i,j) and it's an oxygen cell and not in used_set, we add its amount.

Now, write the code.

I'll use a list to store oxygen cells for lookup.

Let me code step by step.

Note: The problem says "the number of datasets does not exceed 50", and grid is small, so even with many states, it might work.

Let me code.

Important: when we move to a cell, if it's an oxygen cell and we haven't used it, we get the oxygen amount and add it to the oxygen level, then mark it as used.

The oxygen amount is added to the oxygen level before we cap it.

So: new_oxygen_level = (oxygen_level - 1)  # after move
if cell is oxygen and not used:
    new_oxygen_level = min(new_oxygen_level + oxygen_amount, m)
else:
    new_oxygen_level = new_oxygen_level

And the used_set is updated only if it's a new oxygen cell.

Now, let's implement.

We'll use a min-heap.

We'll use a dictionary for visited states.

State: (x, y, oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set)

We can use tuple of (x, y, oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set)

But used_oxygen_set is a frozenset of tuples (i,j)

We'll store visited as a dictionary: key = (x, y, oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set), value = min_cost to reach this state.

Now, let's write the code.


```python
import heapq
import sys

def solve():
    while True:
        line = input().strip()
        if not line:
            continue
        W, H = map(int, line.split())
        if W == 0 and H == 0:
            break
            
        f, m, o = map(int, input().split())
        
        # Read grid
        grid = []
        for _ in range(H):
            row = list(map(int, input().split()))
            grid.append(row)
        
        # Find all oxygen cells
        oxygen_cells = {}  # (i, j) -> amount
        for i in range(W):
            for j in range(H):
                if grid[i][j] > 0:
                    oxygen_cells[(i, j)] = grid[i][j]
        
        # If no oxygen cells, then only soil cells
        # We'll run Dijkstra
        # State: (cost, x, y, oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set)
        # used_oxygen_set is a frozenset of (i,j) for oxygen cells used
        
        # Multiple start points in top row (y=0)
        heap = []
        visited = {}  # key: (x, y, oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set) -> cost
        
        # Start from all cells in row 0
        for x in range(W):
            # Initial state at (x, 0)
            initial_state = (x, 0, o, frozenset())
            if initial_state not in visited:
                heapq.heappush(heap, (0, initial_state[0], initial_state[1], initial_state[2], initial_state[3]))
                visited[initial_state] = 0
        
        min_final_cost = float('inf')
        
        # Directions: left, right, down
        directions = [(-1, 0), (1, 0), (0, 1)]  # left, right, down
        
        while heap:
            cost, x, y, oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set = heapq.heappop(heap)
            
            # If we reached the bottom row
            if y == H-1:
                min_final_cost = min(min_final_cost, cost)
                # We don't stop here because there might be a cheaper path to bottom row
                # But Dijkstra will eventually find all, and we are taking min
                # Continue to explore other paths to bottom row
                continue
                
            # If cost already exceeds excavation cost, skip
            if cost > f:
                continue
                
            # If we have a better cost for this state already, skip
            state_key = (x, y, oxygen_level, used_oxygen_set)
            if state_key in visited and visited[state_key] < cost:
                continue
            visited[state_key] = cost
            
            # Try all three directions
            for dx, dy in directions:
                nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
                # Check bounds
                if nx < 0 or nx >= W or ny >= H:
                    continue
                    
                # Can only move if we have oxygen level > 0
                if oxygen_level <= 0:
                    continue
                    
                # Move to (nx, ny)
                new_oxygen_level = oxygen_level - 1
                new_cost = cost
                
                # Check the cell at (nx, ny)
                cell_val = grid[nx][ny]
                
                # If it's a soil cell (negative value), add digging cost
                if cell_val < 0:
                    new_cost += -cell_val
                # If it's an oxygen cell, and we haven't used it before
                elif cell_val > 0:
                    cell_pos = (nx, ny)
                    if cell_pos not in used_oxygen_set:
                        # Add the oxygen amount
                        oxygen_amount = oxygen_cells[cell_pos]
                        new_oxygen_level = min(new_oxygen_level + oxygen_amount, m)
                        # Create new used set
                        new_used_set = used_oxygen_set | {cell_pos}
                    else:
                        # Already used, so oxygen level remains
                        new_used_set = used_oxygen_set
                else:
                    # Should not happen, cell value should be negative or positive
                    new_used_set = used_oxygen_set
                
                # If new_cost exceeds f, skip
                if new_cost > f:
                    continue
                    
                # Create new state key
                new_state_key = (nx, ny, new_oxygen_level, new_used_set)
                
                # If we haven't seen this state or seen with higher cost, add to heap
                if new_state_key not in visited or visited[new_state_key] > new_cost:
                    visited[new_state_key] = new_cost
                    heapq.heappush(heap, (new_cost, nx, ny, new_oxygen_level, new_used_set))
        
        if min_final_cost == float('inf'):
            print("NA")
        else:
            print(min_final_cost)

solve()
```

If we go (0,0) to (0,1) to (1,1) to (2,1): cost = 100 + 20 + 20 + 60 = 200.
